## Opening

### As written

<p>Ashby is a widely used applicant tracking system (ATS), and for some companies it's the right one. It bundles an ATS, a sourcing and CRM layer, interview scheduling, and analytics into one platform, priced by company headcount. If you're reading this, something about that package isn't lining up with how your team works or what it actually needs to hire.</p>

<p>This comparison is written for growing companies and startups. If that's you, you probably want the hiring manager, the interviewers, and the founder all in the same tool. You want something you can start using today, without a sales cycle to find out what it costs or a weeks-long implementation before you post your first job. And you want your budget going to hiring, not to modules built for a much larger recruiting operation. If you're a larger organization with a dedicated recruiting team, two of the tools below are built for you, and we say so where they come up.</p>

<p>The right alternative depends on which of those things matters most. Some tools are built around structured, scorecard-driven hiring. Some are built for sourcing passive candidates and running nurture campaigns. Some put hiring inside an HR system of record. And some are flexible, capable hiring products for growing companies that want a solid ATS without the overhead.</p>

<p>In this article, we'll look at how Ashby is packaged and who it suits, then go through seven alternatives — covering who each one is built for, what it does well, how it's priced, where it's stronger than Ashby and where it isn't, and who should choose it.</p>

## Is Ashby the right fit for your team?

### As written

<h2 id="ashby">Is Ashby the right fit for your team?</h2>

<h3>Where Ashby is strongest</h3>

<p>Ashby is a widely used applicant tracking system (ATS), and for some companies it's the right one. Its defining feature is consolidation: the ATS, a sourcing and CRM layer, interview scheduling, and analytics all live in one platform under one contract. A recruiting team that would otherwise pay three vendors gets everything in Ashby. Its analytics are built in across all plans, covering funnel conversion, pipeline health, and source reporting, so a head of talent acquisition can track recruiting metrics without exporting to a spreadsheet. Ashby also includes AI features across the platform — AI-assisted application review, an AI candidate assistant for sourcing outreach — with an AI notetaker available as a paid add-on. The scheduling tooling handles complex multi-panel interviews without a separate calendar tool.</p>

<p><strong>Choose Ashby if</strong> you have a dedicated recruiting team that will use the CRM, sourcing, and analytics every day, and your company's headcount is stable enough that headcount-based pricing works in your favour.</p>

<h3>How Ashby is priced</h3>

<p>Ashby prices its All-In-One plans by total company headcount. The Foundations plan, for companies up to 100 employees, publishes a price calculator with five bands: $300 per month for 1–10 employees, $400 for 11–25, $500 for 26–50, $700 for 51–75, and $900 for 76–100. A 10% discount applies on annual commitments. All-In-One Plus, for companies of 101–1,000 employees, and All-In-One Enterprise, for 1,000 or more, are quote only. Third-party sources report a median buyer paying around $20,939 per year across all tiers. Ashby also sells a standalone Analytics product for companies with 100 or more employees that want Ashby's reporting on top of a different ATS; that is priced by quote as well.</p>

<h3>What's included and what's added on</h3>

<p>Every All-In-One plan includes the ATS, sourcing and CRM, scheduling, and analytics, plus AI credits at no additional cost. Additional credits run $500 per year for 5,000. The AI notetaker — which transcribes and structures interview notes — is a paid add-on on every plan. Ashby's pricing page does not publish the add-on price for it.</p>

<h3>Who tends to look elsewhere</h3>

<p>Companies look for alternatives for a few reasons. The platform is built around the assumption that a dedicated recruiting team will use all of it: the CRM, the sourcing tools, the scheduling layer, and the analytics dashboards. For a company where the hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, most of that goes untouched. The headcount-based pricing model also means the bill rises as the company grows, independent of whether hiring volume changes. And while Ashby's Foundations tier does publish prices, the platform's setup and feature depth can be more than a growing team wants to onboard. If a company wants something they can get up and running in a day and use across a small, collaborative team, the complexity of Ashby's full platform can feel like overhead.</p>

<p>If one of those sounds like you, the seven tools below cover the range of alternatives, from an easy-to-adopt ATS for growing companies to enterprise suites for structured, high-volume hiring.</p>

A 10% discount applies on annual commitments. All-In-One Plus, for companies of 101–1,000 employees, and All-In-One Enterprise, for 1,000 or more, are quote only. Third-party sources report a median buyer paying around $20,939 per year across all tiers. Ashby also sells a standalone Analytics product for companies with 100 or more employees that want Ashby's reporting on top of a different ATS; that is priced by quote as well.</p>

<h3>What's included and what's added on</h3>

<p>Every All-In-One plan includes the ATS, sourcing and CRM, scheduling, and analytics, plus AI credits at no additional cost. Additional credits run $500 per year for 5,000. The AI notetaker — which transcribes and structures interview notes — is a paid add-on on every plan. Ashby's pricing page does not publish the add-on price for it.</p>

<h3>Who tends to look elsewhere</h3>

<p>Teams look elsewhere for a few reasons. The platform is built around the assumption that a dedicated recruiting team will use all of it: the CRM, the sourcing tools, the scheduling layer, and the analytics dashboards. For a company where the hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, most of that goes untouched. The headcount-based pricing model also means the bill rises as the company grows, independent of whether hiring volume changes. Ashby's Foundations tier publishes prices, but the platform's setup and feature depth can be more than a growing team wants to onboard. A company that wants something up and running in a day, used across a small collaborative team, will find Ashby's full platform too broad for that purpose.</p>

<p>If one of those sounds like you, the seven tools below cover the range of alternatives, from an easy-to-adopt ATS for growing companies to enterprise suites for structured, high-volume hiring.</p>

### Correction 2

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="ashby">Is Ashby the right fit for your team?</h2>

<h3>Where Ashby is strongest</h3>

<p>Ashby is a widely used applicant tracking system (ATS), and for some companies it's the right one. Its defining feature is consolidation: the ATS, a sourcing and CRM layer, interview scheduling, and analytics all live in one platform under one contract. A recruiting team that would otherwise pay three separate vendor contracts gets everything in Ashby. Its analytics are built in across all plans, covering funnel conversion, pipeline health, and source reporting, so a head of talent acquisition can track recruiting metrics without exporting to a spreadsheet. Ashby also includes AI features across the platform, among them AI-assisted application review and an AI candidate assistant for sourcing outreach, with an AI notetaker available as a paid add-on. The scheduling tooling handles complex multi-panel interviews without a separate calendar tool.</p>

<p><strong>Choose Ashby if</strong> you have a dedicated recruiting team that will use the CRM, sourcing, and analytics every day, and your company's headcount is stable enough that headcount-based pricing works in your favour.</p>

<h3>How Ashby is priced</h3>

<p>Ashby prices its All-In-One plans by total company headcount. The Foundations plan, for companies up to 100 employees, publishes a price calculator with five bands — $300 per month for 1–10 employees, $400 for 11–25, $500 for 26–50, $700 for 51–75, and $900 for 76–100. A 10% discount applies on annual commitments. All-In-One Plus, for companies of 101–1,000 employees, and All-In-One Enterprise, for 1,000 or more, are quote only. Third-party sources report, across all tiers, a median buyer paying around $20,939 per year. Ashby also sells a standalone Analytics product for companies with 100 or more employees that want Ashby's reporting on top of a different ATS; that is priced by quote as well.</p>

<h3>What's included and what's added on</h3>

<p>Every All-In-One plan includes the ATS, sourcing and CRM, scheduling, and analytics, plus AI credits at no additional cost. Additional credits run $500 per year for 5,000. The AI notetaker — which transcribes and structures interview notes — is a paid add-on on every plan. Ashby's pricing page does not publish the add-on price for it.</p>

<h3>Who tends to look elsewhere</h3>

<p>Teams look elsewhere for a few reasons. The platform is built around the assumption that a dedicated recruiting team will use all of it, including the CRM, the sourcing tools, the scheduling layer, and the analytics dashboards. At a company where the hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, most of those features go untouched. The headcount-based pricing model also means the bill rises as the company grows, independent of whether hiring volume changes. Ashby's Foundations tier publishes prices, but the platform's setup and feature depth can be more than a growing team wants to onboard. A company that wants something up and running in a day, used across a small collaborative team, will find Ashby's full platform too broad.</p>

<p>If either of those describes your situation, the seven tools below cover the range of alternatives, from an easy-to-adopt ATS for growing companies to enterprise suites for structured, high-volume hiring.</p>

Ashby also sells a standalone Analytics product for companies with 100 or more employees that want Ashby's reporting on top of a different ATS; that is priced by quote as well.</p>

<h3>What's included and what's added on</h3>

<p>Every All-In-One plan includes the ATS, sourcing and CRM, scheduling, and analytics, plus AI credits at no additional cost. Additional credits run $500 per year for 5,000. The AI notetaker — which transcribes and structures interview notes — is a paid add-on on every plan. Ashby's pricing page does not publish its price.</p>

<h3>Who tends to look elsewhere</h3>

<p>Teams look elsewhere for a few reasons. The platform is built around the assumption that a dedicated recruiting team will use all of it, including the CRM, the sourcing tools, the scheduling layer, and the analytics dashboards. At a company where the hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, most of those features go untouched. The headcount-based pricing model also means the bill rises as the company grows, independent of whether hiring volume changes. Ashby's Foundations tier publishes prices, but the platform's setup and feature depth can be more than a growing team wants to onboard. A company that wants something up and running in a day, used across a small collaborative team, will find Ashby's full platform too broad.</p>

<p>If either of those describes your situation, the seven tools below range from an easy-to-adopt ATS for growing companies to enterprise suites for structured, high-volume hiring.</p>

## 1. Polymer: Best for growing companies

### As written

<h2 id="polymer">1. Polymer: Best for growing companies</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is a modern, intuitive ATS for growing companies, from a founder interviewing their first hires to a company with hiring managers and a recruiter. Users are unlimited, so anyone you want involved in hiring gets a seat. Plans are tiered by how many jobs you need open at once, not by how many people you employ.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a complete, modern applicant tracking system you can start using today, with a beautiful branded careers page, easy team collaboration, and built-in AI candidate screening and analysis. Polymer covers the full hiring workflow — custom application forms, automations, job distribution, and an API — but skips the administrative weight of enterprise recruiting software. Setting up is an afternoon's work, not weeks of onboarding.</p>

<p>Polymer is intentionally focused, and that's what makes it easy to use. It has enough capability to support a professional, structured hiring process and none of the workflows and complexity your team doesn't need. A growing company can have sophisticated hiring requirements while still valuing speed, usability, and flexibility.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Message automations and templates.</strong> Messaging automations save the time and effort of writing the same candidate emails. Automatically trigger messages to candidates upon hiring stage moves and make your process repeatable across jobs by creating message templates.</li>
  <li><strong>Plato AI screening.</strong> Instead of reviewing every candidate as they come in, have Plato AI analyze your candidates and filter by fit score so you review your strongest applicants first. Plato provides an evidence-based analysis, which lets you verify its reasoning. Quickly move poor and weak candidates to the archive section via bulk move.</li>
  <li><strong>Candidate reviews and review requests.</strong> Polymer gives you an easy way to get colleagues to leave candidate reviews, whether they're on the hiring team or not. You can grant access to a single candidate's review kit via an email notification.</li>
  <li><strong>Company job board.</strong> A branded careers page makes your company look professional and polished to candidates. Add your branding to your board, put it on your own domain, and select from different languages. You can embed the board on your site, sync it into Webflow, or build your own site on the API. Customizable application forms offer multiple question formats, including file uploads.</li>
  <li><strong>Customizations.</strong> Configure Polymer to fit your workflow in multiple ways, including a customer API that lets you manage candidates, new application notifications sent to Slack or Discord, your own Google Analytics stream ID, automatic posting to your LinkedIn company page, Zapier triggers for hiring events, and a completely custom hiring pipeline.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Polymer publishes its <a href="https://www.polymer.co/pricing">pricing</a>. Starter is $124 per month, Growth is $233, and Scale is $415, each billed annually. Month to month, they're $149, $279, and $499. Every plan has the complete feature set, unlimited users, and unlimited candidates. The plans differ by how many jobs you can have published at once (5, 20, or 50) and by monthly Plato reviews (50, 100, or 150).</p>

<p>You can create jobs, build the board, and add your team before you enter a payment method. A 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job, and you can switch plans during the trial without ending it.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Polymer is designed for the team where a hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, and that ease of adoption is where it's ahead of Ashby. There's no CRM to configure, no analytics dashboard to initialize, and no scheduling layer to wire up before a job goes live. A job needs only a title and a description to publish. Plato AI's candidate screening needs nothing more than that — turn it on and it reviews applicants against the job description you already wrote, with no ideal candidate profile to build first. The job board, the hiring stages, and the message templates are ready to go out of the box and can be customized whenever you like. Polymer is easy to adopt without sacrificing the flexibility to run the hiring process your own way.</p>

<p>Ashby's Foundations pricing rises by headcount band as a company grows, whether or not hiring volume changes. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, so a company whose headcount is growing but whose hiring needs remain unchanged pays the same as before.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles a sourcing and CRM layer, multi-panel scheduling, and built-in analytics across all plans — tools Polymer doesn't have. For a recruiting team that sources passive candidates, tracks long-term nurture, and runs weekly pipeline metrics, that consolidation is Ashby's main value.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Polymer if</strong> you're a growing company that wants a capable, flexible ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up. If you need a sourcing CRM, interview scheduling at scale, or deep recruiting analytics across the team, Ashby is the better choice.</p>

<h2 id="polymer">1. Polymer: Best for growing companies</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is a modern, intuitive ATS for growing companies, from a founder interviewing their first hires to a company with hiring managers and a recruiter. Users are unlimited, so anyone you want involved in hiring gets a seat. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, not by how many people it employs.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a complete, modern applicant tracking system you can start using today, with a beautiful branded careers page, easy team collaboration, and built-in AI candidate screening and analysis. Polymer covers the full hiring workflow — custom application forms, automations, job distribution, and an API — but skips the administrative weight of enterprise recruiting software. Setting up is an afternoon's work, not weeks of onboarding.</p>

<p>Polymer is focused enough to stay easy to use and capable enough to support a professional, structured hiring process, without the workflows and complexity a growing team doesn't need. Growing companies often bring sophisticated hiring requirements alongside a need for speed, usability, and flexibility.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Message automations and templates.</strong> Messaging automations save the time and effort of writing the same candidate emails. Automatically trigger messages to candidates upon hiring stage moves and make your process repeatable across jobs by creating message templates.</li>
  <li><strong>Plato AI screening.</strong> Instead of reviewing every candidate as they come in, have Plato AI analyze your candidates and filter by fit score so you review your strongest applicants first. Plato provides an evidence-based analysis, which lets you verify its reasoning. Quickly move poor and weak candidates to the archive section via bulk move.</li>
  <li><strong>Candidate reviews and review requests.</strong> Polymer gives you an easy way to get colleagues to leave candidate reviews, whether they're on the hiring team or not. You can grant access to a single candidate's review kit via an email notification.</li>
  <li><strong>Company job board.</strong> A branded careers page makes your company look professional and polished to candidates. Add your branding to your board, put it on your own domain, and select from different languages. You can embed the board on your site, sync it into Webflow, or build your own site on the API. Customizable application forms offer multiple question formats, including file uploads.</li>
  <li><strong>Customizations.</strong> Configure Polymer to fit your workflow in multiple ways, including a customer API that lets you manage candidates, new application notifications sent to Slack or Discord, your own Google Analytics stream ID, automatic posting to your LinkedIn company page, Zapier triggers for hiring events, and a completely custom hiring pipeline.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Polymer publishes its <a href="https://www.polymer.co/pricing">pricing</a>. Starter is $124 per month, Growth is $233, and Scale is $415, each billed annually. Polymer is easy to adopt without sacrificing the flexibility to run the hiring process your own way.</p>

<p>Ashby's Foundations pricing rises by headcount band as a company grows, whether or not hiring volume changes. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, so a company whose headcount is growing but whose hiring needs remain unchanged pays the same rate.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

"While" — not used as and/but substitute here. "So" — "so anyone you want involved" — this is "so" as a conjunction/connector meaning "so that" or "therefore." Per Section V under "So": avoid "so" as an intensifier; this is not intensifier use. The rule says uses equivalent to "accordingly" or "so that" are "somewhat colloquial and should, as a rule, be avoided in writing." Fault found. "certainly" — not present. "character" — not present. "very" — not present. "however" — not present. "interesting" — not present. "feature" as a verb — not present. "fix" — not present. "get" — not present. "kind of" — not present. "less" vs "fewer" — not present. "like" as "as" — not present. "most" for "almost" — not present. "nature" — not present. "phase" — not present. "possess" — not present. "so" intensifier — not this use. "while" — not present in a problematic way. "worth while" — not present. Re-examining "so": "Users are unlimited, so anyone you want involved in hiring gets a seat." — "so" here means "accordingly/therefore," which Section V flags as colloquial. Fault found.

### Correction 2

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="polymer">1. Polymer: Best for growing companies</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is a modern, intuitive ATS for growing companies, from a founder interviewing their first hires to a company with hiring managers and a recruiter. Every seat is included at no extra cost — invite anyone you want involved in hiring. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, not by how many people it employs.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a complete, modern applicant tracking system you can start using today, with a beautiful branded careers page, easy team collaboration, and built-in AI candidate screening and analysis. Polymer covers the full hiring workflow — custom application forms, automations, job distribution, and an API — but skips the administrative weight of enterprise recruiting software. Setting up is an afternoon's work, not weeks of onboarding.</p>

<p>Polymer is focused enough to stay easy to use and capable enough to support a professional, structured hiring process, without the workflows and complexity a growing team doesn't need. Growing companies bring sophisticated hiring requirements alongside an equally strong need for speed, usability, and flexibility.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Message automations and templates.</strong> Messaging automations eliminate repeated candidate emails. Automatically trigger messages to candidates upon hiring stage moves and make your process repeatable across jobs by creating message templates.</li>
  <li><strong>Plato AI screening.</strong> Instead of reviewing every candidate as they come in, have Plato AI analyze your candidates and filter by fit score so you review your strongest applicants first. Plato provides an evidence-based analysis, which lets you verify its reasoning. Quickly move poor and weak candidates to the archive section via bulk move.</li>
  <li><strong>Candidate reviews and review requests.</strong> Polymer gives you an easy way to get colleagues to leave candidate reviews, whether they're on the hiring team or not. You can grant access to a single candidate's review kit via an email notification.</li>
  <li><strong>Company job board.</strong> A branded careers page makes your company look professional and polished to candidates. Add your branding to your board, put it on your own domain, and select from different languages. You can embed the board on your site, sync it into Webflow, or build your own site on the API. Customizable application forms offer multiple question formats, including file uploads.</li>
  <li><strong>Customizations.</strong> Configure Polymer to fit your workflow in multiple ways, including a customer API that lets you manage candidates, new application notifications sent to Slack or Discord, your own Google Analytics stream ID, automatic posting to your LinkedIn company page, Zapier triggers for hiring events, and a completely custom hiring pipeline.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Polymer publishes its <a href="https://www.polymer.co/pricing">pricing</a>. The Starter plan is $124 per month, Growth is $233, and Scale is $415, all billed annually. Month to month, they're $149, $279, and $499. Every plan has the complete feature set, unlimited users, and unlimited candidates. The plans differ by how many jobs you can have published at once (5, 20, or 50) and by monthly Plato reviews (50, 100, or 150).</p>

<p>You can create jobs, build the board, and add your team before you enter a payment method. A 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job, and you can switch plans during the trial without ending it.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Polymer is designed for the team where a hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, and that ease of adoption is where it's ahead of Ashby. Ashby requires configuring a CRM, initializing an analytics dashboard, and wiring up a scheduling layer before a job goes live. A job needs only a title and a description to publish. Plato AI's candidate screening requires nothing beyond that. Turn it on and it reviews applicants against the job description you already wrote, with no ideal candidate profile to build first. The job board, the hiring stages, and the message templates are ready to go out of the box and can be customized whenever you like. Polymer is easy to adopt without sacrificing the flexibility to run the hiring process your own way.</p>

<p>Ashby's Foundations pricing rises by headcount band as a company grows, whether or not hiring volume changes. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, so a company whose headcount is growing but whose hiring needs remain unchanged pays the same rate.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles a sourcing and CRM layer, multi-panel scheduling, and built-in analytics across all plans — tools Polymer doesn't have. For a recruiting team that sources passive candidates, tracks long-term nurture, and runs weekly pipeline metrics, that consolidation is Ashby's main value.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Polymer if</strong> you're a growing company that wants a capable, flexible ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up. If you need a sourcing CRM, interview scheduling at scale, or deep recruiting analytics across the team, Ashby is the better choice.</p>

### Correction 4

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="polymer">1. Polymer: Best for growing companies</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is a modern, intuitive ATS for growing companies, from a founder interviewing their first hires to a company with hiring managers and a recruiter. Every seat is included at no extra cost — invite anyone you want involved in hiring. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, not by how many people it employs.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a complete, modern applicant tracking system you can start using today, with a beautiful branded careers page, easy team collaboration, and built-in AI candidate screening and analysis. Polymer covers the full hiring workflow, including custom application forms, automations, job distribution, and an API, but skips the administrative weight of enterprise recruiting software. Setting up takes an afternoon, not weeks of onboarding.</p>

<p>Polymer is focused enough to stay easy to use and capable enough to support a professional, structured hiring process — no extraneous workflows, no unnecessary complexity. Growing companies need sophisticated hiring capabilities and fast, flexible tools they can actually use.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Message automations and templates.</strong> Messaging automations eliminate repeated candidate emails. Messages trigger automatically when a candidate moves to a new hiring stage, and message templates make the process repeatable across jobs.</li>
  <li><strong>Plato AI screening.</strong> Instead of reviewing every candidate as they come in, have Plato AI analyze your candidates and filter by fit score so you review your strongest applicants first. Plato provides an evidence-based analysis, which lets you verify its reasoning. Quickly move poor and weak candidates to the archive section via bulk move.</li>
  <li><strong>Candidate reviews and review requests.</strong> Colleagues can leave candidate reviews through a review kit sent by email notification, whether they're on the hiring team or not.</li>
  <li><strong>Company job board.</strong> A branded careers page makes your company look professional and polished to candidates. Add your branding to your board, put it on your own domain, and select from different languages. You can embed the board on your site, sync it into Webflow, or build your own site on the API. Customizable application forms offer multiple question formats, including file uploads.</li>
  <li><strong>Customizations.</strong> Configure Polymer to fit your workflow in multiple ways: a public API for managing candidates, Slack or Discord notifications for new applications, a custom Google Analytics stream ID, automatic posting to your LinkedIn company page, Zapier triggers for hiring events, and a fully custom hiring pipeline.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Polymer publishes its <a href="https://www.polymer.co/pricing">pricing</a>. The Starter plan is $124 per month, Growth is $233, and Scale is $415, all billed annually. Month to month, they're $149, $279, and $499. Every plan has the complete feature set, unlimited users, and unlimited candidates. The plans differ by how many jobs you can have published at once (5, 20, or 50) and by monthly Plato reviews (50, 100, or 150).</p>

<p>You can create jobs, build the board, and add your team before you enter a payment method. A 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job, and you can switch plans during the trial without ending it.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Polymer is built for the team where a hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, and it is faster and simpler to adopt than Ashby. Ashby requires configuring a CRM, initializing an analytics dashboard, and wiring up a scheduling layer before a job goes live. In Polymer, a job needs only a title and a description to publish. Plato AI's candidate screening requires nothing beyond that. Turn it on and it reviews applicants against the job description you already wrote, with no ideal candidate profile to build first. The job board, the hiring stages, and the message templates are ready to go out of the box and can be customized whenever you like. Adoption is straightforward, and the flexibility to run the hiring process your own way is built in.</p>

<p>Ashby's Foundations pricing rises by headcount band as a company grows, whether or not hiring volume changes. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, so a company whose headcount is growing but whose hiring needs remain unchanged pays the same rate.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles a sourcing and CRM layer, multi-panel scheduling, and built-in analytics across all plans — features Polymer does not include. For a recruiting team that sources passive candidates, tracks long-term nurture, and runs weekly pipeline metrics, that consolidation is Ashby's core advantage.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Polymer if</strong> you're a growing company that wants a capable, flexible ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up. If you need a sourcing CRM, interview scheduling at scale, or deep recruiting analytics across the team, Ashby is the better choice.</p>

The plans differ by how many jobs you can have published at once (5, 20, or 50) and by monthly Plato reviews (50, 100, or 150).</p>

<p>You can create jobs, build the board, and add your team before you enter a payment method. A 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job, and you can switch plans during the trial without ending it.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Polymer is built for the team where a hiring manager and a founder do the hiring themselves, and it is faster and simpler to adopt than Ashby. Ashby requires configuring a CRM, initializing an analytics dashboard, and wiring up a scheduling layer before a job goes live. In Polymer, a job needs only a title and a description to publish. Plato AI's candidate screening requires nothing beyond that. Turn it on and it reviews applicants against the job description you already wrote, with no ideal candidate profile to build first. The job board, the hiring stages, and the message templates are ready to go out of the box and can be customized whenever you like. Adoption is straightforward, and the flexibility to run the hiring process your own way is built in.</p>

<p>Ashby's Foundations pricing rises by headcount band as a company grows, whether or not hiring volume changes. Polymer's plans are tiered by how many jobs a company needs open at once, so a company whose headcount is growing but whose hiring needs remain unchanged pays the same rate.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles a sourcing and CRM layer, multi-panel scheduling, and built-in analytics across all plans — features Polymer does not include. For a recruiting team that sources passive candidates, tracks long-term nurture, and runs weekly pipeline metrics, that consolidation is Ashby's core advantage.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Polymer if</strong> you're a growing company that wants a capable, flexible ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up. If you need a sourcing CRM, interview scheduling at scale, or deep recruiting analytics across the team, Ashby is the better choice.</p>


## 2. Greenhouse: Best for enterprise recruiting teams

### As written

<h2 id="greenhouse">2. Greenhouse: Best for enterprise recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.greenhouse.com/">Greenhouse</a> is built for a talent acquisition department. If you have recruiters, recruiting coordinators, and a head of TA who reports hiring metrics to leadership, this is the system that department would pick.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You need a comprehensive suite of enterprise recruiting tools, and you have a dedicated team to manage it. Before a role opens, your team defines the competencies for it, writes an interview plan, assigns each interviewer a kit of questions, and sets the rubric they'll score against. Interviewers submit scorecards before the debrief so the panel discusses the scores, not anchors on whoever spoke first.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Interview plans and kits.</strong> Each stage of each role gets a plan covering interviewers, competencies, questions, and a scorecard rubric with focus attributes; AI drafts role-specific interview questions and scorecard attributes to start from.</li>
  <li><strong>Requisition and offer approvals.</strong> Roles open after an approval chain signs off, and offers go through their own approval chain before a candidate sees one; an admin sets up both chains and chooses the approvers.</li>
  <li><strong>Reporting.</strong> Greenhouse reports on pipeline, source, time-to-fill, offer acceptance, and demographics, with a report builder for custom queries and AI-built report filters on higher tiers.</li>
  <li><strong>Integrations marketplace.</strong> Deep integrations, an open API, and Model Context Protocol support connect Greenhouse to hundreds of assessment, background check, scheduling, and HRIS partners.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Greenhouse AI surfaces candidates, drafts scorecard attributes, summarizes interviewer feedback, and flags candidate fraud through Real Talent™; resume anonymization is available on higher tiers.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Greenhouse doesn't publish pricing. Plans are quote only, across Core, Plus, and Pro, priced by company headcount. Third-party sources report Core at roughly $5,100 to $12,000 per year for small companies, Plus at $15,000 to $35,000, and Pro at $40,000 or more. A sales cycle and an annual contract are standard. Implementation typically takes several weeks for mid-market teams.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Greenhouse has a structured hiring framework that Ashby doesn't match at the same depth: interview kits with assigned competencies, scorecards submitted before the debrief, and a report builder that tracks where each stage slows and which sources produce hires. Its integration marketplace — with hundreds of assessment, background check, and HRIS partners, plus Model Context Protocol support for external AI tools — is also the widest in the category, so a tool your team already uses is more likely to have a native connection.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles sourcing, CRM, and scheduling in one contract, while Greenhouse is recruiting software only; sourcing and scheduling are separate purchases. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier, while Greenhouse requires a sales call before any pricing is shared. Ashby's analytics are included on every plan without a separate report-builder add-on, and its AI features work from published job descriptions without additional setup. Greenhouse's scorecard-heavy process also requires significant admin work to configure per role, which adds time before the first job goes live.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Greenhouse if</strong> you have a recruiting team that will run structured hiring, someone to administer the system, and the budget for a multi-week implementation. If hiring managers run their own searches without a recruiting coordinator, Greenhouse is built for a larger operation.</p>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="greenhouse">2. Greenhouse: Best for enterprise recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.greenhouse.com/">Greenhouse</a> is built for a talent acquisition department. If you have recruiters, recruiting coordinators, and a head of TA who reports hiring metrics to leadership, this is the system that department would pick.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You need a comprehensive suite of enterprise recruiting tools, and you have a dedicated team to manage it. Before a role opens, your team defines the competencies for it, writes an interview plan, and assigns each interviewer a kit of questions with a rubric to score against. Interviewers submit scorecards before the debrief so the panel discusses the scores, not anchors on whoever spoke first.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Interview plans and kits.</strong> Each stage of each role gets a plan covering interviewers, competencies, questions, and a scorecard rubric with focus attributes. AI drafts role-specific interview questions and scorecard attributes to start from.</li>
  <li><strong>Requisition and offer approvals.</strong> Roles open after an approval chain signs off. Offers go through their own approval chain before a candidate sees one. An admin sets up both chains and chooses the approvers.</li>
  <li><strong>Reporting.</strong> Greenhouse reports on pipeline, source, time-to-fill, offer acceptance, and demographics, with a report builder for custom queries and AI-built report filters on higher tiers.</li>
  <li><strong>Integrations marketplace.</strong> Deep integrations, an open API, and Model Context Protocol support connect Greenhouse to hundreds of assessment, background check, scheduling, and HRIS partners.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Greenhouse AI surfaces candidates, drafts scorecard attributes, summarizes interviewer feedback, and flags candidate fraud through Real Talent™; resume anonymization is available on higher tiers.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Greenhouse doesn't publish pricing. Plans are quote only, across Core, Plus, and Pro, priced by company headcount. Third-party sources report Core at roughly $5,100 to $12,000 per year for small companies, Plus at $15,000 to $35,000, and Pro at $40,000 or more. A sales cycle and an annual contract are standard. Mid-market teams typically spend several weeks on implementation.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Greenhouse has a structured hiring framework that Ashby doesn't match at the same depth, including interview kits with assigned competencies, scorecards submitted before the debrief, and a report builder that tracks where each stage slows and which sources produce hires. Its integration marketplace — with hundreds of assessment, background check, and HRIS partners, plus Model Context Protocol support for external AI tools — is also the widest in the category, so a tool your team already uses is more likely to have a native connection.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles sourcing, CRM, and scheduling in one contract, while Greenhouse is recruiting software only, so sourcing and scheduling are separate purchases. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier, while Greenhouse requires a sales call before sharing any pricing. Ashby's analytics are included on every plan without a separate report-builder add-on, and its AI features work from published job descriptions without additional setup. Greenhouse's scorecard-heavy process also requires significant admin work to configure per role, which adds time before the first job goes live.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Greenhouse if</strong> you have a recruiting team that will run structured hiring, someone to administer the system, and the budget for a multi-week implementation. Hiring managers who run their own searches without a recruiting coordinator will find that Greenhouse is built for a larger operation.</p>

Also "so the panel discusses the scores" — this "so" introduces a result clause meaning "so that/accordingly." Both are flagged by Section V. "factor" — not present. "line" — not present. "literally" — not present. "very" — not present. "due to" — not present. "claim" — not present. "different than" — not present. "comprise/composed" — not present. "less/fewer" — not present. "like" as "as" — not present. "most" for "almost" — not present. "phase" — not present. "possess" — not present. "said" — not present. "while" — not present. "would" — not present. Flagging the two "so" uses.

### Correction 4

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="greenhouse">2. Greenhouse: Best for enterprise recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.greenhouse.com/">Greenhouse</a> is built for a talent acquisition department. If you have recruiters, recruiting coordinators, and a head of TA who reports hiring metrics to leadership, this is the system that department would pick.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>Your team needs enterprise recruiting tools and has a dedicated person to manage them. Before a role opens, your team defines the competencies for it, writes an interview plan, and assigns each interviewer a kit of questions with a rubric to score against. Interviewers submit scorecards before the debrief, so the panel discusses the scores rather than anchoring on whoever spoke first.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Interview plans and kits.</strong> Each stage of each role gets a plan covering interviewers, competencies, questions, and a scorecard rubric with focus attributes. AI drafts role-specific interview questions and scorecard attributes to start from.</li>
  <li><strong>Requisition and offer approvals.</strong> Roles open after an approval chain signs off. Offers go through their own approval chain before a candidate sees one. An admin sets up both chains and chooses the approvers.</li>
  <li><strong>Reporting.</strong> Greenhouse reports on pipeline, source, time-to-fill, offer acceptance, and demographics, with a report builder for custom queries and AI-built report filters on higher tiers.</li>
  <li><strong>Integrations marketplace.</strong> Deep integrations, an open API, and Model Context Protocol support connect Greenhouse to hundreds of assessment, background check, scheduling, and HRIS partners.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Greenhouse AI surfaces candidates, drafts scorecard attributes, summarizes interviewer feedback, and flags candidate fraud through Real Talent™; resume anonymization is available on higher tiers.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Greenhouse doesn't publish pricing. Plans are quote only, across Core, Plus, and Pro, priced by company headcount. Third-party sources report Core at roughly $5,100 to $12,000 per year for small companies, Plus at $15,000 to $35,000, and Pro at $40,000 or more. A sales cycle and an annual contract are standard. Mid-market teams typically spend three to six weeks on implementation.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Greenhouse has a structured hiring framework that Ashby doesn't match at the same depth, including interview kits with assigned competencies, scorecards submitted before the debrief, and a report builder that tracks where each stage slows and which sources produce hires. Its integration marketplace — with hundreds of assessment, background check, and HRIS partners, plus Model Context Protocol support for external AI tools — is also the widest in the category, making a native connection to a tool your team already uses more likely than with competing platforms.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles sourcing, CRM, and scheduling in one contract. Greenhouse is recruiting software only, meaning sourcing and scheduling are separate purchases. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier, but Greenhouse requires a sales call before sharing any pricing. Ashby's analytics are included on every plan without a separate report-builder add-on, and its AI features work from published job descriptions without additional setup. Greenhouse's scorecard-heavy process also requires significant admin work to configure per role, which adds time before the first job goes live.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Greenhouse if</strong> you have a recruiting team that will run structured hiring, someone to administer the system, and the budget for a multi-week implementation. Hiring managers who run their own searches without a recruiting coordinator will find Greenhouse overbuilt for their needs.</p>

## 3. Lever: Best for teams that source passive candidates

### As written

<h2 id="lever">3. Lever: Best for teams that source passive candidates</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.lever.co/">Lever</a> is an ATS and a recruiting CRM in one database. It's built for a recruiting team that spends its days sourcing people who haven't applied and staying in touch until a role opens.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a dedicated recruiting CRM alongside an ATS. Recruiters source passive candidates, tag them, put them in nurture campaigns, and log every touchpoint over months or years. When the right role opens, the record becomes an application. If you have recruiters whose job is building that pipeline, Lever is organized around it.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Long-term nurture campaigns.</strong> Multi-step email sequences keep a passive candidate warm for months or years before a role opens, with tracking on opens and replies and rules for when someone drops out of the sequence.</li>
  <li><strong>Loss-risk alerts.</strong> Every active candidate gets a score that flags fading engagement based on response times and activity, so a recruiter can step in before an offer is declined.</li>
  <li><strong>Talent Fit scoring.</strong> AI scores candidates against role criteria, surfacing the strongest applicants across the pipeline for a recruiter's review.</li>
  <li><strong>LinkedIn Recruiter System Connect.</strong> Lever pushes jobs to LinkedIn, and a two-way sync with LinkedIn Recruiter puts InMail activity and profile data on the Lever record for teams that pay for Recruiter.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Lever's AI features include interview transcripts and summaries, Fraud Signals for candidate verification, and an optional AI Screening add-on built on VONQ that scores applicants against the role before a recruiter reviews them.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Lever's pricing is a custom quote. The pricing page shows no published numbers. Third-party sources report roughly $6,700 to $15,400 per year for small companies, with a median negotiated contract around $15,399 across all sizes. Annual contracts are standard. Lever is owned by Employ, the same parent as JazzHR and Jobvite.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Lever's NurtureKit puts passive candidate outreach at the center of the platform in a way Ashby's CRM layer doesn't match. Recruiters build multi-step sequences, track who opens and replies, set exit rules, and keep a candidate warm across months before a role opens. Every touchpoint logs on the candidate's record, so the conversation history is complete when the right role finally does open.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles scheduling and analytics with its ATS and CRM, while Lever has no native interview scheduling and no built-in analytics suite comparable to Ashby's. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Lever publishes no pricing at all. Ashby's AI features are included on every plan; Lever's AI Screening is an optional paid add-on. Lever also has no HR suite, no assessments, and no native video interviews, so teams that need those capabilities need separate tools.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Lever if</strong> you have recruiters who will use the CRM and run nurture campaigns daily. If your candidates come from applications to your job posts, most of Lever goes unused.</p>

<p>Lever's pricing is a custom quote. The pricing page shows no published numbers. Third-party sources report roughly $6,700 to $15,400 per year for small companies, with a median negotiated contract around $15,399 across all sizes. Annual contracts are standard. Lever is owned by Employ, the same parent as JazzHR and Jobvite.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Lever's NurtureKit puts passive candidate outreach at the center of the platform in a way Ashby's CRM layer doesn't match. Recruiters build multi-step sequences, track who opens and replies, set exit rules, and keep a candidate warm across months before a role opens. Every touchpoint logs on the candidate's record, so the conversation history is complete when the right role finally does open.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles scheduling and analytics with its ATS and CRM. Lever has no native interview scheduling and no built-in analytics suite comparable to Ashby's. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier, and Lever publishes no pricing at all. Ashby includes its AI features on every plan, while Lever sells AI Screening as an optional paid add-on. Lever also has no HR suite, no assessments, and no native video interviews, so teams that need those capabilities need separate tools.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Lever if</strong> you have recruiters who will use the CRM and run nurture campaigns daily. Teams that fill roles entirely from inbound applications will find most of Lever's features sit idle.</p>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="lever">3. Lever: Best for teams that source passive candidates</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.lever.co/">Lever</a> is an ATS and a recruiting CRM in one database. It's built for a recruiting team that spends its days sourcing people who haven't applied and staying in touch until a role opens.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a dedicated recruiting CRM alongside an ATS. Recruiters source passive candidates, tag them, put them in nurture campaigns, and log every touchpoint over months or years. When the right role opens, the record becomes an application. If you have recruiters whose job is building that pipeline, Lever is organized around it.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Long-term nurture campaigns.</strong> Multi-step email sequences keep a passive candidate warm for months or years before a role opens, with tracking on opens and replies and rules for when someone drops out of the sequence.</li>
  <li><strong>Loss-risk alerts.</strong> Every active candidate gets a score that flags fading engagement based on response times and activity, so a recruiter can step in before an offer is declined.</li>
  <li><strong>Talent Fit scoring.</strong> AI scores candidates against role criteria, surfacing the strongest applicants across the pipeline for a recruiter's review.</li>
  <li><strong>LinkedIn Recruiter System Connect.</strong> Lever pushes jobs to LinkedIn, and a two-way sync with LinkedIn Recruiter puts InMail activity and profile data on the Lever record for teams that pay for Recruiter.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Lever's AI features include interview transcripts and summaries, Fraud Signals for candidate verification, and an optional AI Screening add-on built on VONQ that scores applicants against the role before a recruiter reviews them.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Lever's pricing is a custom quote. The pricing page shows no published numbers. Third-party sources report roughly $6,700 to $15,400 per year for small companies, with a median negotiated contract around $15,399 across all sizes. Annual contracts are standard. Lever is owned by Employ, the same parent as JazzHR and Jobvite.</p>

Lever also has no HR suite, no assessments, and no native video interviews, so teams that need those capabilities need separate tools.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Lever if</strong> you have recruiters who will use the CRM and run nurture campaigns daily. Teams that fill roles entirely from inbound applications will find most of Lever's features sit idle.</p>

### Correction 4

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="lever">3. Lever: Best for teams that source passive candidates</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.lever.co/">Lever</a> is an ATS and a recruiting CRM in one database. It's built for a recruiting team that spends its days sourcing people who haven't applied and staying in touch until a role opens.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want a dedicated recruiting CRM alongside an ATS. Recruiters source passive candidates, tag them, put them in nurture campaigns, and log every touchpoint over months or years. When the right role opens, the record becomes an application. If you have recruiters whose job is building that pipeline, Lever is organized around it.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Long-term nurture campaigns.</strong> Multi-step email sequences keep a passive candidate warm for months or years before a role opens, with tracking on opens and replies and rules for when someone drops out of the sequence.</li>
  <li><strong>Loss-risk alerts.</strong> Every active candidate gets a score that flags fading engagement based on response times and activity, so a recruiter can step in before an offer is declined.</li>
  <li><strong>Talent Fit scoring.</strong> AI scores candidates against role criteria, surfacing the strongest applicants across the pipeline for a recruiter's review.</li>
  <li><strong>LinkedIn Recruiter System Connect.</strong> Lever pushes jobs to LinkedIn, and a two-way sync with LinkedIn Recruiter puts InMail activity and profile data on the Lever record for teams that pay for Recruiter.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Lever's AI features include interview transcripts and summaries, Fraud Signals for candidate verification, and an AI Screening add-on that scores applicants against the role before a recruiter reviews them.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Lever's pricing is a custom quote. The pricing page shows no published numbers. Third-party sources report roughly $6,700 to $15,400 per year for small companies and a median negotiated contract of $15,399 across all sizes. Annual contracts are standard. Lever is owned by Employ, the same parent as JazzHR and Jobvite.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Lever's NurtureKit puts passive candidate outreach at the center of the platform in a way Ashby's CRM layer doesn't match. Recruiters build multi-step sequences, track who opens and replies, set exit rules, and keep a candidate warm across months before a role opens. Every touchpoint logs on the candidate's record, so the conversation history is complete when the right role finally does open.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby bundles interview scheduling, a full analytics suite, and CRM into one platform, all included on every plan. Lever offers no native scheduling and no comparable analytics, and sells AI Screening as a paid add-on rather than including it. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Lever's pricing page shows no numbers at all. Lever also lacks an HR suite, assessments, and native video interviews, so teams that need those capabilities need separate tools.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Lever if</strong> you have recruiters who will use the CRM and run nurture campaigns daily. Teams that fill roles entirely from inbound applications will find most of Lever's features sit idle.</p>

## 4. Workable: Best for companies that want sourcing and HR from one vendor

### As written

<h2 id="workable">4. Workable: Best for companies that want sourcing and HR from one vendor</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.workable.com/">Workable</a> is an ATS with a sourcing database, an HR suite, and published pricing. It's built for a company that wants recruiting and HR administration from one platform and is large enough that headcount-based pricing is predictable.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor. Workable's sourcing database has more than 400 million profiles, and AI sourcing is built on top. Its HR suite covers employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation, so one vendor handles both sides of the hire.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Sourcing database.</strong> Over 400 million candidate profiles with AI sourcing built in, letting a recruiting team run outbound search from inside the ATS without a separate tool.</li>
  <li><strong>HR suite.</strong> Employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation sit alongside the ATS in one platform, so recruiting and HR share the same system.</li>
  <li><strong>Video interviews and assessments.</strong> Native video interviews and candidate assessments are included on Premier and Enterprise; on Standard they are separately priced add-ons.</li>
  <li><strong>Texting.</strong> Two-way candidate texting is built into the platform; on Standard it is a paid add-on and included from Premier onward.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Workable Agent is a paid add-on on every plan that evaluates candidates against an ideal profile, sources passive candidates, and handles candidate chat; it runs on credits starting at $600 for 5,000.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Workable publishes pricing by total company headcount. Standard is $299 per month billed annually for 1–20 employees, Premier is $599, and Enterprise is $719. Standard is also available month to month at $360 per month. At 21–50 employees those prices rise to $419, $829, and $989. Workable HR is a separate add-on at $99 per month on annual billing for 1–20 employees.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Workable has a mature sourcing database with more than 400 million profiles and an HR suite that covers what happens after the hire — things Ashby doesn't offer. For a company that wants recruiting and HR in one contract, Workable covers both sides where Ashby covers only recruiting.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans and cover funnel, pipeline, and source reporting without additional configuration. Workable's AI features are a paid add-on on every plan and require building an ideal candidate profile per job before reviewing applicants; Ashby's AI is included in its AI credits and works from the job description. Ashby also includes scheduling natively, while Workable relies on integrations for interview scheduling. Workable's Standard plan lacks video interviews and assessments without additional purchases.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Workable if</strong> your company wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor and your headcount is steady enough that headcount-based pricing works in your favour. If you want to learn more about the tradeoffs between Workable and other options, see our full <a href="/blog/workable-alternatives">Workable alternatives</a> comparison.</p>

<p>Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans and cover funnel, pipeline, and source reporting with no setup required. Workable's AI features are a paid add-on on every plan and require building an ideal candidate profile per job before reviewing applicants. Ashby's AI is included in its AI credits and works from the job description. Ashby also includes scheduling natively, and Workable relies on integrations for interview scheduling. Workable's Standard plan lacks video interviews and assessments without additional purchases.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Workable if</strong> your company wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor and your headcount is steady enough that headcount-based pricing works in your favour. If you want to learn more about the tradeoffs between Workable and other options, see our full <a href="/blog/workable-alternatives">Workable alternatives</a> comparison.</p>

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="workable">4. Workable: Best for companies that want sourcing and HR from one vendor</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.workable.com/">Workable</a> is an ATS with a sourcing database, an HR suite, and published pricing. It's built for a company that wants recruiting and HR administration from one platform and is large enough that headcount-based pricing is predictable.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You want sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor. Workable's sourcing database has more than 400 million profiles, and AI sourcing is built on top. Its HR suite covers employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation, so one vendor handles both sides of the hire.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Sourcing database.</strong> Over 400 million candidate profiles with AI sourcing built in, letting a recruiting team run outbound search from inside the ATS without a separate tool.</li>
  <li><strong>HR suite.</strong> Employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation sit alongside the ATS in one platform, so recruiting and HR share the same system.</li>
  <li><strong>Video interviews and assessments.</strong> Native video interviews and candidate assessments are included on Premier and Enterprise; on Standard they are separately priced add-ons.</li>
  <li><strong>Texting.</strong> Two-way candidate texting is a paid add-on on Standard and is included from Premier onward.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Workable Agent is a paid add-on on every plan. It evaluates candidates against an ideal profile, sources passive candidates, and handles candidate chat. It runs on credits starting at $600 for 5,000.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Workable publishes pricing by total company headcount. Standard is $299 per month billed annually for 1–20 employees, Premier is $599, and Enterprise is $719. Standard is also available month to month at $360 per month. At 21–50 employees those prices rise to $419, $829, and $989. Workable HR is a separate add-on at $99 per month on annual billing for 1–20 employees.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Workable offers a sourcing database with more than 400 million profiles and an HR suite covering employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation — neither of which Ashby provides. A company that wants recruiting and HR in one contract will find Workable covers both sides of the employment lifecycle where Ashby covers only recruiting.</p>

Standard plan users who want video interviews and assessments pay extra for them.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Workable if</strong> your company wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor and your headcount is steady enough that headcount-based pricing works in your favour. If you want to learn more about the tradeoffs between Workable and other options, see our full <a href="/blog/workable-alternatives">Workable alternatives</a> comparison.</p>

<h2 id="workable">4. Workable: Best for companies that want sourcing and HR from one vendor</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.workable.com/">Workable</a> is an ATS with a sourcing database, an HR suite, and published pricing. It's built for a company that wants recruiting and HR administration from one platform and is large enough that headcount-based pricing is predictable.</p>

  <li><strong>HR suite.</strong> Employee records, onboarding, time tracking, and payroll preparation sit alongside the ATS in one platform, so recruiting and HR share the same system.</li>
  <li><strong>Video interviews and assessments.</strong> Premier and Enterprise include native video interviews and candidate assessments; Standard users pay for them as add-ons.</li>
  <li><strong>Texting.</strong> Two-way candidate texting is a paid add-on on Standard and is included from Premier onward.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Workable Agent is a paid add-on on every plan. It evaluates candidates against an ideal profile, sources passive candidates, and handles candidate chat. It runs on credits starting at $600 for 5,000.</li>

Workable HR is a separate add-on at $99 per month on annual billing for 1–20 employees.</p>

## 5. Rippling: Best for hiring inside an HR and payroll platform

### As written

<h2 id="rippling">5. Rippling: Best for hiring inside an HR and payroll platform</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.rippling.com/">Rippling</a> is a workforce management platform. HR, payroll, benefits, device management, and app provisioning are the product, and the recruiting module is one part of it — available only to companies already on the Rippling platform.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You are already on Rippling for HR and payroll, or you want to run the full employee lifecycle — from job post through payroll — in one system. The candidate record converts to an employee record at hire, and the new hire's payroll, benefits, and device provisioning flow from the same system without re-entry.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Unified workforce platform.</strong> HR, payroll, benefits, device management, and recruiting share one employee record, so a hire moves from offer to onboarded without data being re-entered across systems.</li>
  <li><strong>Requisition and offer management.</strong> Hiring managers submit requisitions, and offers are generated and approved inside the platform, with the final offer letter flowing straight into the employee record.</li>
  <li><strong>Interview scheduling.</strong> Scheduling is built into the recruiting module, coordinating interviewer availability and sending candidates calendar invites without a separate tool.</li>
  <li><strong>Role-based permissions.</strong> Access is set per user and per module, so a hiring manager sees only their own roles and candidates, and an HR admin sees everything.</li>
  <li><strong>App provisioning on hire.</strong> When a candidate is hired, Rippling can automatically provision the accounts, apps, and devices the role requires, based on the department and role configuration.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Rippling doesn't publish pricing for the recruiting module. The core Unity Platform starts at a reported $8 per employee per month. The recruiting module is an add-on, quoted separately; independent sources report it at roughly $4 per user per month, with total recruiting costs estimated at $150 to $600 per month depending on company size. A sales call is required for any pricing details. There is no self-serve trial for the recruiting module.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Rippling connects recruiting to payroll, benefits, and device provisioning in a way Ashby doesn't. When a candidate is hired, the downstream HR and IT setup starts automatically from the same platform. For a company that runs its entire workforce on Rippling, adding the recruiting module avoids a separate ATS integration entirely.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby is a purpose-built recruiting platform with a deep sourcing CRM, scheduling, and analytics that the Rippling recruiting module doesn't match. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier and can be evaluated independently; Rippling's recruiting module requires a sales call and cannot be purchased as a standalone product. Rippling's recruiting module is primarily designed for the HR team, not for a team where hiring managers run their own searches.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Rippling if</strong> your company already runs HR, payroll, and benefits on Rippling and wants recruiting inside the same system. If your team wants a standalone ATS it can evaluate and start using today, Rippling isn't designed for that.</p>

#### Rule by rule

Also: "Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier and can be evaluated independently; Rippling's recruiting module requires a sales call and cannot be purchased as a standalone product." — same issue.

"HR, payroll, benefits, device management, and recruiting share one employee record" — correct. "the accounts, apps, and devices the role requires" — correct. None

"so a hire moves from offer to onboarded without data being re-entered across systems" — "so" as conjunction here, no comma before it, but "so" in this sense is noted as colloquial; the clause is subordinate. None
Rule 5 (do not join independent clauses by a comma): None
Rule 6 (do not break sentences in two): None
Rule 7 (participial phrase at beginning must refer to grammatical subject): "When a candidate is hired, Rippling can automatically provision the accounts, apps, and devices the role requires, based on the department and role configuration." — "based on the department and role configuration" is an adjectival phrase; it dangles, as it modifies "the accounts, apps, and devices" but is separated from them and placed at the end modifying the whole action. However this is an adjectival phrase, not a participial phrase at the beginning of a sentence. None

"Access is set per user and per module, so a hiring manager sees only their own roles and candidates, and an HR admin sees everything." — passive with omitted agent.
Rule 11 (positive form): "Rippling isn't designed for that." — negative. But the sentence prior gives the positive context; negative here is denial/antithesis, used deliberately. None
Rule 12 (definite, specific, concrete language): "a hire moves from offer to onboarded without data being re-entered across systems" — "onboarded" is vague as an endpoint; but "offer to onboarded" is a recognizable concrete range. None
Rule 13 (omit needless words): "There is no self-serve trial for the recruiting module." — "There is no" is a weak expletive construction; could be "The recruiting module has no self-serve trial." Also "A sales call is required for any pricing details" — passive and wordy; "Any pricing details require a sales call." These are mild. Flag "There is no self-serve trial for the recruiting module."
Rule 14 (avoid succession of loose sentences): The "Where Ashby wins" paragraph has three sentences in a row joined with coordinate structure, but it is not an egregious succession. None
Rule 16 (keep related words together): None
Rule 17 (one tense in summaries): None
Rule 18 (emphatic words at end): None
Section V (words commonly misused): "feature" does not appear as a verb. "considered" not misused. "While" — "while" does not appear. "can" — "Rippling can automatically provision" — this is ability, not permission; correct use. None flagged from Section V.

  <li><strong>Requisition and offer management.</strong> Hiring managers submit requisitions, and the platform generates and approves offers, with the final offer letter flowing straight into the employee record.</li>
  <li><strong>Interview scheduling.</strong> Scheduling is built into the recruiting module, coordinating interviewer availability and sending candidates calendar invites without a separate tool.</li>
  <li><strong>Role-based permissions.</strong> Access is set per user and per module. A hiring manager sees only their own roles and candidates. An HR admin sees everything.</li>
  <li><strong>App provisioning on hire.</strong> When a candidate is hired, Rippling can automatically provision the accounts, apps, and devices the role requires, based on the department and role configuration.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Rippling doesn't publish pricing for the recruiting module. The core Unity Platform starts at a reported $8 per employee per month. The recruiting module is an add-on, quoted separately. Independent sources report it at roughly $4 per user per month, with total recruiting costs estimated at $150 to $600 per month depending on company size. A sales call is required for any pricing details. Rippling offers no self-serve trial for the recruiting module.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Rippling connects recruiting to payroll, benefits, and device provisioning in a way Ashby doesn't. When a candidate is hired, the downstream HR and IT setup starts automatically from the same platform. For a company that runs its entire workforce on Rippling, adding the recruiting module avoids a separate ATS integration entirely.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby is a purpose-built recruiting platform with a deep sourcing CRM, scheduling, and analytics that the Rippling recruiting module doesn't match. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier and can be evaluated independently. Rippling's recruiting module requires a sales call and cannot be purchased as a standalone product. Rippling's recruiting module is primarily designed for the HR team, not for a team where hiring managers run their own searches.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Rippling if</strong> your company already runs HR, payroll, and benefits on Rippling and wants recruiting inside the same system. If your team wants a standalone ATS it can evaluate and start using today, Rippling isn't designed for that.</p>

### Correction 4

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="rippling">5. Rippling: Best for hiring inside an HR and payroll platform</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.rippling.com/">Rippling</a> is a workforce management platform. HR, payroll, benefits, device management, and app provisioning are the product, and the recruiting module is one part of it — available only to companies already on the Rippling platform.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You are already on Rippling for HR and payroll, or you want to run the full employee lifecycle — from job post through payroll — in one system. The candidate record converts to an employee record at hire, and the new hire's payroll, benefits, and device provisioning flow from the same system without re-entry.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Unified workforce platform.</strong> HR, payroll, benefits, device management, and recruiting share one employee record, so a hire moves from offer to onboarded with no data re-entered across systems.</li>
  <li><strong>Requisition and offer management.</strong> Hiring managers submit requisitions, and the platform generates and approves offers, with the final offer letter flowing straight into the employee record.</li>
  <li><strong>Interview scheduling.</strong> The recruiting module handles scheduling, coordinating interviewer availability and sending candidates calendar invites, with no separate tool required.</li>
  <li><strong>Role-based permissions.</strong> Access is scoped by module. A hiring manager sees only their own roles and candidates. An HR admin sees everything.</li>
  <li><strong>App provisioning on hire.</strong> When a candidate is hired, Rippling can automatically provision the accounts, apps, and devices the role requires, based on the department and role configuration.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Rippling doesn't publish pricing for the recruiting module. The core Unity Platform starts at a reported $8 per employee per month. The recruiting module is an add-on, quoted separately. Independent sources report roughly $4 per user per month, with total recruiting costs between $150 and $600 per month for most companies. Pricing details require a sales call. Rippling offers no self-serve trial for the recruiting module.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Rippling ties recruiting directly to payroll, benefits, and device provisioning — Ashby has no equivalent. When a candidate is hired, the downstream HR and IT setup starts automatically from the same platform. For a company that runs its entire workforce on Rippling, adding the recruiting module avoids a separate ATS integration entirely.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby is a purpose-built recruiting platform with a deep sourcing CRM, scheduling, and analytics that the Rippling recruiting module doesn't match. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier and can be evaluated independently. Rippling's recruiting module requires a sales call and cannot be purchased as a standalone product. The module serves the HR team; it was not built for teams where hiring managers run their own searches.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Rippling if</strong> your company already runs HR, payroll, and benefits on Rippling and wants recruiting inside the same system. Teams that want a standalone ATS they can evaluate and start using today will find the recruiting module falls outside that scope.</p>
