## 6. Teamtailor: Best for employer branding-first recruiting

### As written

<h2 id="teamtailor">6. Teamtailor: Best for employer branding-first recruiting</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.teamtailor.com/">Teamtailor</a> is an ATS built around the careers site. Employer branding, candidate experience, and DEI tooling are where it focuses, and the applicant tracking sits inside that.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>Your company's careers site is the primary way candidates find you, and you want the ATS vendor to own the whole experience. Teamtailor's careers site builder is drag-and-drop, with no coding required, and it supports video content, employee testimonials, and culture pages alongside the job listings.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Careers site builder.</strong> A drag-and-drop editor builds careers pages with video, employee stories, culture sections, and job listings without a developer or a separate CMS; teams control the full candidate experience from one place.</li>
  <li><strong>Anonymous hiring.</strong> Candidate names and photos are hidden from the pipeline until a recruiter chooses to reveal them, reducing early-stage bias in the review process.</li>
  <li><strong>DEI tools.</strong> EEO surveys, anonymous application review, and diversity reporting are built into the platform for teams that track representation across the funnel.</li>
  <li><strong>Chrome sourcing extension.</strong> Recruiters add profiles from LinkedIn and other sites directly into the candidate database from a browser extension, without leaving the page they're on.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> AI Co-pilot, available on higher tiers, drafts job descriptions, generates interview questions, and summarizes candidate profiles; a candidate chatbot on the careers site handles applicant questions automatically.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Teamtailor doesn't publish pricing. Plans are quote only. Third-party sources report Starter at roughly $300 to $400 per month, Growth at $600 to $1,200, and Enterprise at $1,500 or more, with mid-market annual contracts commonly in the range of $6,000 to $12,000.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Teamtailor's careers site builder is the product's center of gravity in a way Ashby's job board is not. Teams build multi-page careers sites with culture content, video, and employee stories without touching code. Anonymous hiring and EEO reporting are built in rather than bolted on, which matters for teams that make DEI a formal part of the screening process.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans, while Teamtailor's reporting is lighter on lower tiers. Ashby's scheduling, CRM, and sourcing are part of the core platform; Teamtailor's sourcing is limited to a Chrome extension with no native CRM layer. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Teamtailor requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Teamtailor's AI Co-pilot is gated to higher tiers, so smaller plans get no AI assistance in the pipeline.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Teamtailor if</strong> your careers site is the primary channel for attracting candidates and your team wants to own that experience end to end without a developer. If deep recruiting analytics and CRM matter more than branding, Ashby is the stronger fit.</p>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="teamtailor">6. Teamtailor: Best for employer branding-first recruiting</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.teamtailor.com/">Teamtailor</a> is an ATS built around the careers site. Employer branding, candidate experience, and DEI tooling are where it focuses, and the applicant tracking sits inside that.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>Your company's careers site is the primary way candidates find you, and you want the ATS vendor to own the whole experience. Teamtailor's careers site builder is drag-and-drop, with no coding required, and it supports video content, employee testimonials, and culture pages alongside the job listings.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Careers site builder.</strong> A drag-and-drop editor builds careers pages with video, employee stories, culture sections, and job listings without a developer or a separate CMS. Teams control the full candidate experience from one place.</li>
  <li><strong>Anonymous hiring.</strong> Candidate names and photos are hidden from the pipeline until a recruiter chooses to reveal them, reducing early-stage bias in the review process.</li>
  <li><strong>DEI tools.</strong> EEO surveys, anonymous application review, and diversity reporting are built into the platform for teams that track representation across the funnel.</li>
  <li><strong>Chrome sourcing extension.</strong> Recruiters add profiles from LinkedIn and other sites directly into the candidate database from a browser extension, without leaving the page they're on.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> AI Co-pilot, available on higher tiers, drafts job descriptions, generates interview questions, and summarizes candidate profiles. A candidate chatbot on the careers site handles applicant questions automatically.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Teamtailor requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Plans are quote only. Third-party sources report Starter at roughly $300 to $400 per month, Growth at $600 to $1,200, and Enterprise at $1,500 or more, with mid-market annual contracts commonly in the range of $6,000 to $12,000.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Teamtailor's careers site builder is the product's center of gravity in a way Ashby's job board is not. Teams build multi-page careers sites with culture content, video, and employee stories without touching code. Anonymous hiring and EEO reporting are built in rather than bolted on, which matters for teams that make DEI a formal part of the screening process.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans; Teamtailor's reporting is lighter on lower tiers. Ashby's scheduling, CRM, and sourcing are part of the core platform, while Teamtailor's sourcing is limited to a Chrome extension with no native CRM layer. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier. Teamtailor requires a sales conversation for any pricing information, and its AI Co-pilot is gated to higher tiers, leaving smaller plans with no AI assistance in the pipeline.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Teamtailor if</strong> your careers site is the primary channel for attracting candidates and your team wants to own that experience end to end without a developer. If deep recruiting analytics and CRM matter more than branding, Ashby is the stronger fit.</p>

#### Fixer's draft

<h2 id="teamtailor">6. Teamtailor: Best for employer branding-first recruiting</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.teamtailor.com/">Teamtailor</a> is an ATS built around the careers site. It focuses on employer branding, candidate experience, and DEI tooling, with applicant tracking built inside that framework.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>Your company's careers site is the primary way candidates find you, and you want the ATS vendor to own the whole experience. Teamtailor's careers site builder is drag-and-drop, with no coding required, and it supports video content, employee testimonials, and culture pages alongside the job listings.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Careers site builder.</strong> A drag-and-drop editor builds careers pages with video, employee stories, culture sections, and job listings without a developer or a separate CMS. The candidate experience, from first visit to application, runs through a single platform.</li>
  <li><strong>Anonymous hiring.</strong> Candidate names and photos are hidden from the pipeline until a recruiter chooses to reveal them, reducing early-stage bias in the review process.</li>
  <li><strong>DEI tools.</strong> EEO surveys, anonymous application review, and diversity reporting are built into the platform for teams that track representation across the funnel.</li>
  <li><strong>Chrome sourcing extension.</strong> Recruiters add profiles from LinkedIn and other sites directly into the candidate database without navigating away.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> AI Co-pilot, available on higher tiers, drafts job descriptions, generates interview questions, and summarizes candidate profiles. A candidate chatbot on the careers site handles applicant questions automatically.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Teamtailor requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. All plans are priced on quote. Third-party sources report Starter at roughly $300 to $400 per month, Growth at $600 to $1,200, and Enterprise at $1,500 or more, with mid-market annual contracts commonly reaching $6,000 to $12,000.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Teamtailor's careers site builder is the product's center of gravity in a way Ashby's job board is not. Teams build multi-page careers sites with culture content, video, and employee stories without touching code. Anonymous hiring and EEO reporting ship as core platform features, ready for teams that make DEI a formal part of the screening process.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans, but Teamtailor's reporting is lighter on lower tiers. Ashby's scheduling, CRM, and sourcing are part of the core platform, but Teamtailor's sourcing is a Chrome extension with no native CRM layer. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier, but Teamtailor requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Teamtailor's AI Co-pilot is gated to higher tiers, leaving smaller plans with no AI assistance in the pipeline.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Teamtailor if</strong> your careers site is the primary channel for attracting candidates and your team wants to own that experience end to end without a developer. If deep recruiting analytics and CRM matter more than branding, Ashby is the stronger fit.</p>

## 7. Pinpoint: Best for mid-market in-house recruiting teams

### As written

<h2 id="pinpoint">7. Pinpoint: Best for mid-market in-house recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.pinpointhq.com/">Pinpoint</a> is an in-house recruiting platform for companies running several hiring streams at once — desk-based and frontline, early-career and executive, agency-sourced and direct — all in one system.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You're a mid-market company managing several different hiring streams, working with recruiting agencies, and running employee referrals alongside direct applications. Pinpoint covers all three, with an agency portal, a referral program, an onboarding module, and approval chains keyed to salary band.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Lifecycle automation.</strong> If-this-then-that rules across the candidate lifecycle auto-advance on an assessment score, send templated communications at each step, and route an offer through the approval chain for its salary band.</li>
  <li><strong>Agency portal.</strong> External recruiters get their own login to submit candidates into the pipeline, see where each one stands, and track fees, without needing an internal seat.</li>
  <li><strong>Onboarding module.</strong> Hires move from the offer stage into an onboarding module with task lists, document collection, and start-date reminders for the new hire and the manager.</li>
  <li><strong>Background and reference checks.</strong> A recruiter orders both from the candidate's record and tracks them to completion inside the platform, with results attached to the candidate.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Available on the Enterprise tier, Pinpoint includes a hiring copilot that answers plain-language questions and drafts communications, a notetaker that transcribes interviews, a candidate chatbot on the careers site, and candidate match scores against criteria a recruiter sets.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Pinpoint doesn't publish a rate card. Contracts are annual and there's no free trial. Third-party reviewers report Growth at roughly $345 per month on annual billing and Enterprise at $1,200 or more, with annual costs estimated between $4,600 and $15,000. AI features, blind screening, and API access are all on Enterprise only.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Pinpoint has an agency portal and an employee referral program that Ashby doesn't. External recruiters submit candidates through their own login, and employees refer through a tracked program. Lifecycle automation moves candidates on assessment scores and routes each offer for approval by salary band. Background checks, reference checks, and video interviews are included in the platform without a separate purchase.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby has a sourcing database and CRM built for outbound recruiting, while Pinpoint tracks the applicants who come to you and has no outbound sourcing layer. Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans; Pinpoint gates AI and advanced analytics to Enterprise. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Pinpoint requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Growth-tier Pinpoint teams get no AI assistance in the hiring pipeline at all.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Pinpoint if</strong> you're a mid-market in-house recruiting team managing multiple hiring streams, working with agencies, and prepared to sign an annual contract. If your team is a few people hiring for a handful of roles at a time, it's built for a bigger operation than yours.</p>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="pinpoint">7. Pinpoint: Best for mid-market in-house recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.pinpointhq.com/">Pinpoint</a> is an in-house recruiting platform for companies running several hiring streams at once — desk-based and frontline, early-career and executive, agency-sourced and direct — all in one system.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You're a mid-market company managing several different hiring streams, working with recruiting agencies, and running employee referrals alongside direct applications. Pinpoint covers all three, with an agency portal, a referral program, an onboarding module, and approval chains keyed to salary band.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Lifecycle automation.</strong> If-this-then-that rules across the candidate lifecycle auto-advance on an assessment score, send templated communications at each step, and route an offer through the approval chain for its salary band.</li>
  <li><strong>Agency portal.</strong> External recruiters get their own login to submit candidates into the pipeline, see where each one stands, and track fees, without needing an internal seat.</li>
  <li><strong>Onboarding module.</strong> Hires move from the offer stage into an onboarding module with task lists, document collection, and start-date reminders for the new hire and the manager.</li>
  <li><strong>Background and reference checks.</strong> A recruiter orders both from the candidate's record and tracks them to completion inside the platform, with results attached to the candidate.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Available on the Enterprise tier, Pinpoint includes a hiring copilot that answers plain-language questions and drafts communications, a notetaker that transcribes interviews, a candidate chatbot on the careers site, and candidate match scores against criteria a recruiter sets.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Pinpoint doesn't publish a rate card. Contracts are annual, and Pinpoint offers no free trial. Third-party reviewers report Growth at roughly $345 per month on annual billing and Enterprise at $1,200 or more, with annual costs estimated between $4,600 and $15,000. AI features, blind screening, and API access are all on Enterprise only.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Pinpoint has an agency portal and an employee referral program that Ashby doesn't. External recruiters submit candidates through their own login, and employees refer through a tracked program. Lifecycle automation moves candidates on assessment scores and routes each offer for approval by salary band. Background checks, reference checks, and video interviews are included in the platform without a separate purchase.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby has a sourcing database and CRM built for outbound recruiting, while Pinpoint tracks the applicants who come to you and has no outbound sourcing layer. Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans; Pinpoint gates AI and advanced analytics to Enterprise. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Pinpoint requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Growth-tier Pinpoint teams get no AI assistance in the hiring pipeline at all.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Pinpoint if</strong> you're a mid-market in-house recruiting team managing multiple hiring streams, working with agencies, and prepared to sign an annual contract. If your team is a few people hiring for a handful of roles at a time, Pinpoint is built for a bigger operation than yours.</p>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="pinpoint">7. Pinpoint: Best for mid-market in-house recruiting teams</h2>

<p><a href="https://www.pinpointhq.com/">Pinpoint</a> is an in-house recruiting platform for companies running several hiring streams at once — desk-based and frontline, early-career and executive, agency-sourced and direct — all in one system.</p>

<h3>Why consider it</h3>

<p>You're a mid-market company with multiple hiring streams running at once — agency-sourced roles, employee referrals, and direct applications. Pinpoint covers all three, with an agency portal, a referral program, an onboarding module, and approval chains keyed to salary band.</p>

<h3>Key features</h3>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Lifecycle automation.</strong> If-this-then-that rules across the candidate lifecycle auto-advance on an assessment score, send templated communications at each step, and route an offer through the approval chain for its salary band.</li>
  <li><strong>Agency portal.</strong> External recruiters get their own login to submit candidates into the pipeline, see where each one stands, and track fees, without needing an internal seat.</li>
  <li><strong>Onboarding module.</strong> Hires move from the offer stage into an onboarding module with task lists, document collection, and start-date reminders for the new hire and the manager.</li>
  <li><strong>Background and reference checks.</strong> A recruiter orders both from the candidate's record, tracks them to completion inside the platform, and receives results attached to the candidate.</li>
  <li><strong>Generative AI.</strong> Available on the Enterprise tier, Pinpoint includes a hiring copilot that answers plain-language questions and drafts communications, a notetaker that transcribes interviews, a candidate chatbot on the careers site, and candidate match scores against criteria a recruiter sets.</li>
</ul>

<h3>Pricing</h3>

<p>Pinpoint requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. Contracts are annual, and Pinpoint offers no free trial. Third-party reviewers report Growth at roughly $345 per month on annual billing and Enterprise at $1,200 or more, with annual costs estimated between $4,600 and $15,000. AI features, blind screening, and API access are all on Enterprise only.</p>

<h3>Where it beats Ashby</h3>

<p>Pinpoint has an agency portal and an employee referral program that Ashby doesn't. External recruiters submit candidates through their own login, and employees refer through a tracked program. Lifecycle automation moves candidates on assessment scores and routes each offer for approval by salary band. Background checks, reference checks, and video interviews are included in the platform without a separate purchase.</p>

<h3>Where Ashby wins</h3>

<p>Ashby has a sourcing database and CRM built for outbound recruiting. Pinpoint tracks the applicants who come to you and has no outbound sourcing layer. Ashby's analytics are built in across all plans; Pinpoint gates AI and advanced analytics to Enterprise. Ashby publishes prices for its Foundations tier; Pinpoint requires a sales conversation for any pricing information. On the Growth tier, Pinpoint offers no AI assistance in the hiring pipeline.</p>

<h3>Best fit</h3>

<p><strong>Choose Pinpoint if</strong> you're a mid-market in-house recruiting team managing multiple hiring streams, working with agencies, and prepared to sign an annual contract. If your team is small and hiring for only a handful of roles at a time, Pinpoint is built for teams running a much heavier hiring load.</p>

## How to choose an Ashby alternative

### As written

<h2 id="how-to-choose">How to choose an Ashby alternative</h2>

<p>Work through these before you book any demos. Most teams can rule out half the list in ten minutes.</p>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Recruiting team structure.</strong> Do you have dedicated recruiters, or do hiring managers do the hiring themselves? Ashby, Greenhouse, and Lever assume a talent acquisition operation. Polymer assumes the hiring manager is doing the hiring alongside a founder or a small team.</li>
  <li><strong>Need for sourcing or a recruiting CRM.</strong> If outbound sourcing is part of a recruiter's daily work, Lever, Ashby, or Workable has the tooling for it. If you hire from inbound applications, most of that tooling goes unused.</li>
  <li><strong>Hiring volume and concurrent roles.</strong> How many jobs are open at once, and how many applications does each one get? Polymer's plans are sized by published jobs (5, 20, or 50). Ashby's plans are sized by headcount. Pinpoint and Teamtailor both require annual contracts sized for a mid-market operation.</li>
  <li><strong>Workflow complexity and flexibility.</strong> Do you need approval chains by salary band, multi-stream pipelines, and an agency portal? Or custom hiring stages and application forms per job? Pinpoint is the former. Polymer is the latter. For <a href="/blog/talent-acquisition-vs-recruitment">talent acquisition teams</a> running structured processes, Greenhouse or Ashby fits better.</li>
  <li><strong>Implementation tolerance.</strong> Greenhouse is a multi-week implementation. Ashby's Foundations tier is self-serve but has a full platform to configure. Polymer is running the day you sign up.</li>
  <li><strong>AI requirements.</strong> Decide whether you want AI screening included, as an add-on, or not at all. Polymer includes it on every plan with nothing to configure. Ashby includes AI credits on every plan. Workable sells AI separately. Pinpoint gates it to Enterprise. Rippling has no recruiting-focused AI.</li>
  <li><strong>Integration and HR needs.</strong> If you want recruiting inside your HR and payroll system, Rippling is worth evaluating. If you need a wide integration marketplace, Greenhouse has the most partners. Polymer's API manages candidates and job data on every plan.</li>
  <li><strong>Budget and pricing model.</strong> Ask how the price moves as your company grows. Headcount-based pricing (Ashby, Greenhouse, Workable) rises with the company. Job-based pricing (Polymer) rises with hiring volume. Flat annual contracts (Pinpoint) are predictable but require a commitment. Published pricing (Polymer, Workable) lets you evaluate without a sales call.</li>
</ul>

#### Corrected

<h2 id="how-to-choose">How to choose an Ashby alternative</h2>

<p>Work through these before you book any demos. Most teams can rule out half the list in ten minutes.</p>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Recruiting team structure.</strong> Do you have dedicated recruiters, or do hiring managers do the hiring themselves? Ashby, Greenhouse, and Lever assume a talent acquisition operation. Polymer assumes the hiring manager is doing the hiring alongside a founder or a small team.</li>
  <li><strong>Need for sourcing or a recruiting CRM.</strong> If outbound sourcing is part of a recruiter's daily work, Lever, Ashby, or Workable has the tooling for it. If you hire from inbound applications, most of that tooling goes unused.</li>
  <li><strong>Hiring volume and concurrent roles.</strong> How many jobs are open at once, and how many applications does each one get? Polymer's plans are sized by published jobs (5, 20, or 50). Ashby's plans are sized by headcount. Pinpoint and Teamtailor both require annual contracts sized for a mid-market operation.</li>
  <li><strong>Workflow complexity and flexibility.</strong> Do you need approval chains by salary band, multi-stream pipelines, and an agency portal? Or custom hiring stages and application forms per job? Pinpoint is the former. Polymer is the latter. For <a href="/blog/talent-acquisition-vs-recruitment">talent acquisition teams</a> running structured processes, Greenhouse or Ashby fits better.</li>
  <li><strong>Implementation tolerance.</strong> Greenhouse is a multi-week implementation. Ashby's Foundations tier is self-serve but has a full platform to configure. Polymer is ready to use the day you sign up.</li>
  <li><strong>AI requirements.</strong> Decide whether you want AI screening included, as an add-on, or not at all. Polymer includes AI screening on every plan with no configuration required. Ashby includes AI credits on every plan. Workable sells AI separately. Pinpoint gates it to Enterprise. Rippling has no recruiting-focused AI.</li>
  <li><strong>Integration and HR needs.</strong> If you want recruiting inside your HR and payroll system, Rippling is worth evaluating. If you need a wide integration marketplace, Greenhouse has the most partners. Polymer's API manages candidates and job data on every plan.</li>
  <li><strong>Budget and pricing model.</strong> Ask how the price moves as your company grows. Headcount-based pricing (Ashby, Greenhouse, Workable) rises with the company. Job-based pricing (Polymer) rises with hiring volume. Flat annual contracts (Pinpoint) are predictable but require a commitment. Published pricing (Polymer, Workable) lets you evaluate without a sales call.</li>
</ul>

  <li><strong>Need for sourcing or a recruiting CRM.</strong> If outbound sourcing is part of a recruiter's daily work, Lever, Ashby, or Workable has the tooling for it. If you hire from inbound applications, most of that tooling goes unused.</li>
  <li><strong>Hiring volume and concurrent roles.</strong> How many jobs are open at once, and how many applications does each one get? Polymer's plans are sized by published jobs (5, 20, or 50). Ashby's plans are sized by headcount. Pinpoint and Teamtailor both require annual contracts sized for a mid-market operation.</li>
  <li><strong>Workflow complexity and flexibility.</strong> Do you need approval chains by salary band, multi-stream pipelines, and an agency portal? Or custom hiring stages and application forms per job? Pinpoint is the former. Polymer is the latter. For <a href="/blog/talent-acquisition-vs-recruitment">talent acquisition teams</a> running structured processes, Greenhouse or Ashby fits better.</li>
  <li><strong>Implementation tolerance.</strong> Greenhouse requires a multi-week implementation. Ashby's Foundations tier is self-serve but puts a full platform in front of you to configure. Polymer is ready to use the day you sign up.</li>
  <li><strong>AI requirements.</strong> Decide whether you want AI screening included, as an add-on, or not at all. Polymer includes AI screening on every plan with no configuration required. Ashby includes AI credits on every plan. Workable sells AI separately, Pinpoint gates it to Enterprise, and Rippling has no recruiting-focused AI.</li>
  <li><strong>Integration and HR needs.</strong> If you want recruiting inside your HR and payroll system, Rippling is worth evaluating. If you need a wide integration marketplace, Greenhouse has the most partners. Polymer's API, available on every plan, manages candidates and job data.</li>
  <li><strong>Budget and pricing model.</strong> Ask how the price moves as your company grows. Headcount-based pricing (Ashby, Greenhouse, Workable) rises with the company. Job-based pricing (Polymer) rises with hiring volume. Flat annual contracts (Pinpoint) are predictable but require a commitment. Published pricing (Polymer, Workable) lets you evaluate without a sales call.</li>
</ul>

## Which Ashby alternative is right for you?

### As written

<h2 id="verdict">Which Ashby alternative is right for you?</h2>

<p>How much recruiting software does your company actually need? Every product on this list differs in which features it focuses on, how complex those features are, and how much process is built in around them. What your team needs from those three is what decides which one fits. Some of that built-in process is overkill for a smaller team and causes confusion and slow adoption. Some of it is necessary structure for a larger recruiting operation.</p>

<p>If you're a growing company that wants a capable, intuitive ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up, <a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is the fit. Set up your jobs, board, and team before you enter any payment details, and the 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job.</p>

<p>Greenhouse fits a dedicated recruiting team running structured, scorecard-driven hiring. Lever suits recruiters building a passive candidate pipeline with long-term nurture. Workable is for a company that wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor. Rippling fits a company already running its workforce on the Rippling platform. Teamtailor is for a team where the careers site and employer brand are the primary candidate channel. Pinpoint is for a mid-market in-house recruiting team managing multiple streams, agencies, and referrals under one flat-rate annual contract.</p>

<p>Not every recruiting tool fits every team. Each product on this list targets a different kind of team — lean and intuitive on one end, deep and process-heavy on the other. For a company that wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor, Workable is the natural choice. Rippling fits a company already running its workforce on the Rippling platform. Teamtailor fits a team whose primary candidate channel is the careers site and employer brand. ### Final

<h2 id="verdict">Which Ashby alternative is right for you?</h2>

<p>Not every recruiting tool fits every team. Each product on this list targets a different kind of team — lean and intuitive on one end, deep and process-heavy on the other. The right fit depends on how many open roles you run at once, how many people touch each hire, and how formal your process needs to be. Smaller teams often find that built-in process slows adoption rather than helping it. Larger recruiting operations need that structure to keep hiring consistent.</p>

<p>If you're a growing company that wants a capable, intuitive ATS that's ready to use the day you sign up, <a href="https://www.polymer.co/">Polymer</a> is the fit. Set up your jobs, board, and team before you enter any payment details, and the 14-day free trial starts when you publish your first job.</p>

<p>Greenhouse fits a dedicated recruiting team running structured, scorecard-driven hiring. Lever is built for recruiters whose strategy centers on passive candidates and long-term nurture. For a company that wants sourcing, recruiting, and HR administration from one vendor, Workable is the natural choice. Rippling fits a company already running its workforce on the Rippling platform. Teamtailor fits a team whose primary candidate channel is the careers site and employer brand. Pinpoint suits a mid-market in-house recruiting team that manages multiple streams, agencies, and referrals under one flat-rate annual contract.</p>
